# Phase 3 deep-dive findings — confirmed vs refuted

> **⚓ Frame — read first (reanchor):** this repo is a **general forensic engine for financial fragility & mispricing across the _whole economy_** — hidden / mismatched / circular leverage and valuation-run-ahead-of-cash-flow, found with **no sector prior**. **AI / data-center is _case zero_, not the object** (the method is sector- and era-agnostic — cf. fiber 1999, shale 2014). **Operating mode: maximum exhaustiveness — broad AND deep, uncapped, ≥ $1M-substance floor, US-primary (international by connectedness-to-core × data-accessibility); acting resource-constrained is NEVER correct; the only stop is physics (data that doesn't publicly exist → estimate it from proxies).** Full scope & doctrine: [total_ecosystem_dive.md](total_ecosystem_dive.md) · [README](../README.md).

Per-target forensic profile + adversarial verdict, paired from the deep-dive fan-out. **450 entities** with findings (332 adversarially verified): **174 confirmed real fragility**, **158 refuted as false positives**. Severity: {'already-distressed': 41, 'high': 24, 'moderate': 88, 'low': 144, 'none': 150, 'unknown': 3}. Machine-readable: [phase3_findings.json](phase3_findings.json).

### INNOVATE Corp. (NYSE: VATE; formerly HC2 Holdings), CIK 0001006837 — **already-distressed** (press_reported)
CONFIRMED real distress, not an EBITDA-trough artifact. INNOVATE is a parent holdco whose ~$662M debt stack is not serviced by its own cash flows. FY2025 consolidated interest expense of $89.0M against consolidated Adjusted EBITDA of only $67.2M = coverage ~0.75x (and ~0.3x on EBIT); the company structurally does not earn its interest bill and reported a $60.6M net loss. ### Sotherly Hotels Inc. ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: The XBRL distress flag is REAL, not an artifact — but it is already-realized history, not a forward mispricing. Pre-merger Sotherly was a chronically over-leveraged post-COVID hotel REIT: ~$315.8M debt (June 30, 2025) at 5.89% WACD against trough EBITDA, producing interest coverage ~0.7x (EBIT ~$15.6M < interest) and net-debt/EBITDA in the mid-teens — i.e. operating earnings could not service interest. Equity was a sliver (shareholder equity ~$36.8M, negative tangible book ~-$3.20/sh) on a ~$320M debt stack (D/E ~870%), and the company had stacked-up preferred dividend arrears (~$23.9M; Series B/C/D dividends deferred Oct/Nov 2025 and future dividends suspended). A hotel-level loan default (SOHO Atlanta LLC, ~$38.0M, lender Wilmington Trust threatening foreclosure) and ~$87.8M of near-term balloon maturities against only ~$9.4M unrestricted cash made the capital structure non-self-financing. The distress resolved through a control event rather than a default cascade: KW Kingfisher LLC (Kemmons Wilson Hospitality Partners + Ascendant Capital JV) acquired the common for $2.25/share on Feb 12, 2026, refinancing with a NEW $308M Apollo senior loan (matures ~2029) plus up to $45M Ascendant mezzanine (matures ~2030) — i.e. leverage was re-struck, not deleveraged. NET: confirm distress = TRUE; forward fragility trade = LARGELY EXHAUSTED. The common is extinguished; the SOHON Series D preferred had a change-of-control conversion right at $16.642013/share cash (conversion date Mar 20, 2026; ~820,066 Series D shares tendered for ~$13.65M on Mar 25, 2026) and the preferred is being voluntarily delisted from Nasdaq to OTC (Form 25 ~Apr 7, 2026) while the company continues SEC reporting. Residual fragility now sits at the asset/loan level inside the new Apollo/Ascendant-financed private vehicle, not in tradeable SOHO/SOHON securities.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: already-distressed\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Going concern doubt disclosed (FY2025 10-K / Q2 2025); Preferred dividends deferred Oct/Nov 2025 and FUTURE preferred dividends suspended; ~$23.9M cumulative arrears; SOHO Atlanta LLC loan (~$38.0M) in payment default; Wilmington Trust threatening foreclosure; Interest coverage <1x (~0.7-0.77x); EBIT below interest expense; ~$87.8M near-term balloon maturities vs ~$9.4M unrestricted cash; Negative tangible book value (~-$3.20/share); D/E ~870%; 2025 AFFO/share guidance cut to $0.34-$0.37 from $0.57-$0.61; RevPAR -5.4% YoY; Distressed take-private at $2.25/common share (Feb 12, 2026) — small equity value vs debt; Voluntary delisting of Series B/C/D preferred from Nasdaq to OTC (Form 25 ~Apr 7, 2026); Change-of-control conversion of Series D preferred; ~820,066 shares tendered for ~$13.65M (Mar 25, 2026)\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### System1, Inc. ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED real distress, not a data artifact — but the canary has already fired AND been partially defused, so the residual mispricing is about execution/dilution, not an imminent surprise default. The economy-wide scan got the direction right but the magnitudes wrong in a way that matters: it reported \"near-term (<=2027) debt ~$0.00B,\" which is the opposite of the truth. At 12/31/2025 essentially the ENTIRE debt stack was near-term — a ~$260M term loan maturing July 2027 and a $50M revolver maturing Jan 27, 2027 (gross principal ~$305M, total debt net $305.1M = $76.7M current + $228.4M LT). That is a 2027 maturity wall against only $86.9M cash and $3.0M net working capital. The XBRL trigger (net-debt/EBITDA ~11.3x, interest coverage ~0.37x) is directionally real but uses a trough/GAAP-style EBITDA of ~$20M; on management's Adjusted EBITDA of $41.9M, cash interest coverage is ~1.5x ($41.9M AdjEBITDA / $27.6M interest) — thin but not zero. The genuine fragility drivers are: (1) revenue in structural decline (FY25 revenue -23% to $266.1M; Q4 -31%), driving (2) an explicit auditor/management going-concern (10-K filed 3/11/2026, doubt for the 12 months following), and (3) extreme counterparty concentration — Google service agreements = 67% of FY25 revenue, a single-point-of-failure for a search-arbitrage model already pressured by AI-driven changes to search. Net loss was $81.2M in FY25. The key update the scan did not capture: on 5/29/2026 System1 executed a distressed debt exchange with ALL term/revolver lenders, swapping ~$302.6M of 2027-maturity debt for a $150M term loan due Jan 2031 (SOFR+5%), $39.3M of convertible preferred (7% PIK-able cumulative dividend), and a ~$31.4M cash payment — cutting indebtedness >$160M and pushing the maturity wall out to 2031. So the 2027 cliff is being termed-out, but at the cost of (a) heavy equity dilution via the convertible preferred (subject to a shareholder vote, close expected Q3 2026), (b) a ~$31.4M cash drain at a company with only ~$87M cash, and (c) an unresolved litigation overhang: certain lenders are suing over the August 2024 reorganization (assets dropped into System1 Holdings LLC) as a potential fraudulent transfer/conveyance — a contingent liability the exchange is partly intended to settle but which is not fully extinguished. Net read: this is an already-distressed micro-cap (S&P CCC+/negative, prior \"SD\"/selective-default after a distressed buyback), correctly flagged. It is NOT mispriced as a hidden short — the market and ratings already reflect distress. The residual fragility is binary execution risk on the exchange closing + dilution, plus the structural risk that the Google-dependent revenue base keeps shrinking faster than the new $150M/SOFR+5% loan can be serviced (interest alone ~$11-12M/yr on the new loan vs. AdjEBITDA $41.9M and falling).\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: already-distressed\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Explicit going-concern: management/auditor 'substantial doubt' for 12 months following the FY25 10-K (filed ~3/11/2026); FY25 revenue -23% YoY to $266.1M; Q4 2025 revenue -31% YoY to $51.9M — accelerating structural decline; GAAP net loss $81.2M FY25; S&P CCC+ with negative outlook; prior downgrade to 'SD' (selective default) after a distressed debt buyback; 2027 maturity wall pre-exchange: $50M revolver (Jan 27 2027) + ~$260M term loan (July 2027) vs $86.9M cash, $3.0M net working capital; Distressed debt exchange executed 5/29/2026 (sub-par: ~$302.6M old debt for $150M new loan + $39.3M conv. preferred + $31.4M cash) — a de facto sub-par restructuring; Lender litigation alleging the August 2024 asset reorganization (into System1 Holdings LLC) was a fraudulent transfer/conveyance — contingent liability; 67% revenue concentration in Google — single-counterparty dependence; Revolver fully drawn ($50M) — no undrawn liquidity backstop; Adjusted EBITDA -54% in Q4 2025 ($8.2M vs prior) despite full-year +9% — deceleration into year-end; Material dilution pending: convertible preferred requires shareholder approval; close contingent and not yet effective\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### Mobile Infrastructure Corp ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED real distress, but the fragility fulcrum is NOT what the scan saw. The scan's \"near-term (<=2027) debt ~$0.00B\" is a DATA ARTIFACT: it missed the ~$25.9M Harvest Small Cap Partners related-party Line of Credit because its maturity has been serially rolled in 3-month increments (Sept 2025 -> Dec 31 2025 -> Mar 31 2026 -> June 30 2026), so on any given filing date it never reads as a fixed 2026/2027 maturity. THAT LOC is the actual canary. As of Q1 2026: ~$25.9M principal + ~$5.9M accrued interest at a punitive 15.0% PIK-style rate (paid only at maturity) against just $8.5M cash. Management's own 10-K/10-Q carry an explicit 'substantial doubt about the ability to continue as a going concern' — they state they 'do not currently have sufficient cash on hand, liquidity or projected cash flows to repay' the LOC, and assert (probable, not certain) that asset sales + further extension alleviate it. The XBRL coverage/leverage flags are also genuine, not a pure non-cash artifact: even on Adjusted EBITDA (~$14.3M FY25) vs ~$19M interest expense, coverage is only ~0.75x, and on GAAP it is ~0.11-0.57x. The company is cash-burning (FY25 net loss $23.7M, widening; Q1'26 net loss $7.8M) and must sell properties on an 'orderly basis' to service a short-dated related-party balloon. Mitigants that argue AGAINST acute blow-up risk: (1) the LOC lender is an insider affiliate (Jeffrey Osher / No Street Capital), which makes another extension highly likely rather than a hard default; (2) ~$382M total assets / ~$159M equity provide asset coverage and the $100M, 36-month asset-rotation program is funding paydowns (e.g., Honolulu garage sold $16.5M -> $8.1M CMBS + $4.5M LOC paydown); (3) the bulk of debt — $75.5M CMBS at ~4.15% and the $100M ABS at 4.15% (anticipated repayment 2030, legal final 2055) — is genuinely termed out to 2030, so there is no large term-debt wall. Net read: a thinly-capitalized, cash-burning micro-cap whose solvency hinges on (a) continued goodwill of a related-party lender and (b) successful asset sales into a held-for-sale program. Real distress, small dollars (~$0.19-0.21B total debt), and fully self-disclosed — a confirmed canary, not a hidden mispricing.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: already-distressed\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Explicit 'substantial doubt about ability to continue as a going concern' in FY2025 10-K and Q1 2026 10-Q; Management admits insufficient cash/liquidity/projected cash flows to repay the LOC at maturity; FY2025 net loss $23.7M, widened from $8.4M in 2024; Q1 2026 net loss $7.8M vs $4.3M prior-year quarter; Cash fell to ~$8.5M (Q1'26) from ~$15.3M (YE25 incl. restricted); 15.0% per-annum related-party LOC with interest deferred to maturity (PIK-like) — penalty-grade pricing signaling limited external credit access; Serial 3-month maturity extensions of the LOC (Sept 2025 -> Dec 2025 -> Mar 2026 -> June 2026) indicate inability to repay or refinance externally; Forced asset sales (Honolulu garage, $30M+ cumulative under $100M rotation program) used to fund debt paydowns — deleveraging by liquidation; GAAP interest coverage ~0.11x; even Adjusted EBITDA coverage <1x; Dividends on Series A/Series 1 Preferred being settled partly via cash redemptions funded by the LOC — financing distributions with borrowed money\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? Is the high coupon a one-off / penalty / mis-extraction? Already refinanced or covenant-cured? Is the leverage matched by hard assets or stable contracted revenue? Is the flagged signal a data artifact? ### Xerox Holdings Corp ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED real distress — NOT a data artifact, though the headline 18.4x net-debt/EBITDA overstates it. The flag was triggered off a trough-EBITDA period (~$0.18B), and ~$1.4B of the ~$4.4B total debt is self-liquidating finance-asset debt (the captive lessor funds customer equipment leases at ~7:1 against ~$1.4B of finance receivables) that should not be levered against operating EBITDA. The honest read is core/operating-company debt of ~$3.0B against ~$500M run-rate EBITDA = ~6-7x core leverage (mgmt and Moody's both peg ~6-7x pro forma). That is still severe. The thesis is corroborated independently of any XBRL ratio by HARD distress events: (1) Moody's downgraded XRX three notches to Caa2 (negative outlook) on weak post-Lexmark performance and refinancing risk; (2) S&P downgraded to 'SD' (selective default) with the 2028 notes cut to 'D' after Xerox repurchased ~$101M face of 2028 notes for ~$45M — a completed below-par/distressed exchange where noteholders got less than promised; (3) a 13% senior bridge note ($125M, due end Q2 2026) is a penalty-rate instrument signaling stressed access to capital; (4) two consecutive years of ~$1B GAAP net losses (FY24 -$1.32B incl. $1.0B goodwill impairment; FY25 -$1.03B); (5) operating cash flow collapsed $511M→$224M FY24→FY25 and went NEGATIVE in Q1 2026 (-$144M OCF, -$165M FCF); (6) the dividend was slashed from $0.125/qtr to $0.025/qtr in 2025 to preserve cash for debt paydown. The genuine question is not whether XRX is fragile (it is, and rating agencies agree) but whether the ~$300M of near-term (≤2027) maturities are manageable — they are bridgeable via the TPG Angelo Gordon IP-JV ($400M+ liquidity) and $637M cash, so the acute risk is the larger $649M 2028 / $500M 2029 wall against a shrinking, FCF-negative core that may force a broader restructuring before 2028. The scan's '≤2027 ~$0.00B near-term' figure is WRONG — there is ~$300M due through 2027 including the 13% bridge.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: already-distressed\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Moody's downgrade three notches to Caa2, negative outlook (post-Lexmark weakness + refinancing risk); S&P downgrade to 'SD' selective default; 2028 notes cut to 'D' on below-par repurchase ($101M face bought for $45M); Completed distressed exchange — noteholders received less than the original promise (S&P determination); 13% penalty-rate senior bridge notes ($125M, due Q2 2026) — signals stressed capital access; Two consecutive years of ~$1B GAAP net loss (FY24 -$1.32B incl. $1.0B goodwill impairment; FY25 -$1.03B); Operating cash flow collapse $511M→$224M (FY24→FY25); NEGATIVE OCF -$144M and FCF -$165M in Q1 2026; Common dividend slashed from $0.125 to $0.025/qtr in 2025 to fund debt paydown; Secularly declining print market; S&P projects 4-6% revenue decline for 2026; pro forma revenue -7.6%; Goodwill impairment $1.0B in 2024 (asset write-down); Moody's warns XRX may need to RESTRUCTURE debt if it cannot refinance 2028 maturities\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### Bausch Health Companies Inc. (Specialty pharma / healthcare, tier beam)\nTHESIS: The scan's headline ($32.29B gross / $18B due <=2027 / 11% coupon / circular financing) is materially STALE and partially a consolidation/refi artifact. Reality as of Q1 2026: consolidated total debt is ~$20.4-20.8B (long-term debt and other = $20.764B at 3/31/2026), NOT $32B. Of that, ~$4.6B is BLCO debt that is consolidated for accounting (88% stake) but is non-recourse to the BHC stub (BLCO has its own credit agreement and was designated an unrestricted subsidiary in Nov 2022). The recourse RemainCo debt is roughly $15-16B. The $32B figure double-counts pre-refinancing principal and/or BLCO debt — a real data artifact. The '$18B refi wall <=2027' is largely RESOLVED: two 2025 refinancings totaling ~$9.6B (including a $4.0-4.4B 10.00% notes-due-2032 issue and a $1.6B Dec-2025 exchange) pushed maturities out so that <$700M remains through 2027; the wall now sits in 2028-2032. The 11.0% coupon is REAL but being retired — the ~$1.77B 11.00% Senior Secured Notes due 2028 were exchanged into new 10.00% notes due 2032. The 'circular/related-party' flag maps to the legitimate ~$2.2B intercompany note and 88% BLCO ownership structure, not fraudulent circular financing. So three of four seed hypotheses are downgraded. THE GENUINE FRAGILITY THAT REMAINS: (1) Leverage is still ~6.5x net debt/EBITDA (~$20B net debt vs ~$3.1B consolidated adj. EBITDA; stub EBITDA ~$2.7B), deep junk (Moody's Caa2, S&P CCC/CCC+). (2) Coupons on new money are ~10% — refinancing is happening at distressed rates, so each term-out raises cash interest (interest expense rose to $1.604B in 2025, +$216M YoY) and erodes the very FCF used to deleverage. (3) The earnings engine has a hard structural deadline: Xifaxan (largest product, ~$1.6-2B revenue, Salix segment) faces authorized/settled generic entry from Teva/Sun/Sandoz in Jan 2028, and the Phase 3 RED-C trial to expand the label FAILED in Jan 2026, removing the key offset. A $1.4B impairment was booked in Q1 2026. So this is a real, slow-moving deleveraging-vs-patent-cliff race, not an imminent default. Solvency depends on (a) continued refi access at ~10%, and (b) monetizing the BLCO stake (worth multiples of the stub's market cap) before the 2028 Xifaxan cliff bites. NOT an imminent canary; it is a chronically-stressed, already-distressed-rated credit whose fragility is correctly real but whose magnitude was overstated ~1.6x by the scan.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: moderate\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Deep speculative-grade ratings: Moody's Caa2 CFR, S&P CCC/CCC+, negative-leaning outlook; New-money refinancing executed at ~10.00% coupons (distressed pricing); prior 11.00% 2028 notes outstanding; S&P had previously rated 'SD' (selective default) on a past exchange — history of distressed-exchange treatment; $1.4B impairment booked Q1 2026; Xifaxan label-expansion Phase 3 (RED-C) FAILED Jan 2026 — removes key patent-cliff offset; Settled generic Xifaxan entry Jan 2028 threatens largest revenue/EBITDA contributor; Interest expense rose to $1.604B in 2025 (+$216M YoY), outpacing some op-cash improvement; Net debt/EBITDA ~6.5x — covenant headroom and refi optionality remain the swing factors; Chronic overhang since Valeant-era; equity trades as event-driven/levered option on BLCO monetization\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? The \"$0.33B gross\" figure almost certainly double-counts gross facility issuances and rolled/converted/repaid tranches across the year (the company churned dozens of short-dated notes — e.g., $51.2M gross notes-payable proceeds AND $57.3M repayments in 2025 alone). The real fragility is structural and self-evident: (1) Going concern + $767M accumulated deficit at 3/31/26, growing ~$32M PER QUARTER. (2) Cash burn: $62.5M used in operations FY2025 vs only $13.1M unrestricted cash at year-end ($36.2M restricted is largely pledged Bitcoin collateral) — a sub-3-month operating runway absent continuous capital raising. (3) Survival is 100% dilution-funded: $156.3M of financing inflows in 2025 ($125M common-stock sales + $32.4M Series B preferred + convertibles), and the share count exploded from 323M to 370M Class A shares in a SINGLE quarter (Q1 2026), authorized Class A raised from 500M to 2.5 BILLION shares on 4/16/26 — classic death-spiral mechanics with VWAP/floor-price convertibles. (4) The flagship $68.75M AGREE construction loan (~65-75% of notes payable) is in technical default: required April 2026 principal payments and ~$2M interest reserves remained UNFUNDED as of the Q1 filing (lenders have granted forbearance/waivers but not waived the default). (5) Preferred stack is a permanent cash drain: 13.00% Series D and 10.00% Series E perpetual preferreds plus a 9.5% Series H ($100M facility with Ault & Co.); $8.6M preferred dividends paid in 2025, $2.5M in Q1 2026, against negative operating cash flow. The max-coupon 15% signature is real (15% term notes \"in default,\" SJC 15% convertible, and effective rates up to 36-77% once OID/forbearance fees are amortized — the 12% Ault term note carries a 77% EFFECTIVE rate), confirming distressed-priced paper. ### Celularity Inc (Other / unclassified, tier canary)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED FRAGILE — a genuine canary, but the economy-wide scan MIS-SIZED it and MIS-LABELED the mechanism. The scan's \"gross debt ~$0.06B / leverage 0.01\" is a data artifact: the scraper caught only the small senior-secured / convertible promissory-note tranches ($2.0M-$8.4M each) and missed the structural distress. The real picture at 12/31/2025 (FY2025 10-K, filing-verified): total liabilities $145.4M vs total assets $107.3M = stockholders' DEFICIT of -$38.1M (vs +$8.8M positive equity a year earlier); working-capital deficit of -$68.4M; cash of only $6.2M against a FY2025 net loss of $91.7M and net cash used in operations of $13.3M; accumulated deficit $991.5M. Explicit going-concern: 10-K states cash is 'less than will be required to fund operations for a period of 12 months,' Q1-2026 headcount/salary cuts already executed. The most important canary mechanism the scan didn't see: in Aug 2025 Celularity 'retired all $41.6M senior secured debt' NOT by paying it down but by selling its core IP to Celeniv Pte. Ltd. and licensing it back — converting ~$41.6M of on-balance-sheet secured debt into a $31.7M long-term + $2.1M short-term LICENSE obligation (off the 'debt' line but still a hard claim on cash, with amortization-of-licensing-premium and ~$6.4M loss-on-extinguishment hitting the P&L). So the company looks de-levered on a naive debt screen while being economically MORE encumbered (it sold the crown-jewel IP). Starr, now from Philip Barach (DoubleLine co-founder) via the Dec-2025 $10M ($7.0M senior + up to $5.0M convertible @ $1.66) deal. ### Lexmark, as US holdings, Lexmark International, Inc. (Other / unclassified, tier canary)\nTHESIS: REJECT the canary flag as a MIS-ATTRIBUTION DATA ARTIFACT, but the underlying distress is REAL at the parent. The scan attached a $0.25B / 13.0% coupon \"canary\" to \"Lexmark\" because the entity name was extracted as a counterparty/guarantor from Xerox Holdings' July 1, 2025 acquisition-financing 8-K. Three corrections: (1) The $250M / 13.00% Senior Notes due 2030 were \"completed [by] Holdings ... issued by Holdings\" — i.e., XEROX HOLDINGS CORPORATION is the issuer, not Lexmark. The 13% coupon is Xerox acquisition financing, not a Lexmark tranche. (2) \"Lexmark\" in the deal-counterparty roster is the ACQUISITION TARGET (Lexmark International II, LLC / Lexmark International, Inc.), bought from Ninestar/PAG/Shanghai Shouda for ~$1.5B inclusive of net debt, closing July 1, 2025. (3) Lexmark has NO surviving standalone public debt: \"Substantially all of the net proceeds of the Incremental Term Loans were used to repay ... a portion of Lexmark's outstanding debt under the Lexmark Credit Agreement\" — Lexmark's pre-existing debt was extinguished at close. There is no standalone Lexmark issuer that can \"fail first.\" HOWEVER, the genuine fragility object is the CONSOLIDATED ACQUIRER, Xerox Holdings: post-Lexmark it is ~6x gross-debt/EBITDA, downgraded by S&P to 'SD' (selective default) in Q1 2026 after a below-par senior unsecured buyback, by Moody's to Caa2, with negative core free operating cash flow (~ -$170-200M) and a 2028 refi wall amid a structurally declining print market. The 13% (and 13.5% 2nd-lien / 10.25% 1st-lien) coupons are real distress pricing — they just belong to Xerox, not to Lexmark. Net: the \"canary\" is correctly pointing at distress, but the label is on the wrong legal entity; the signal should be re-keyed to Xerox Holdings (CIK 0001770450).\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: already-distressed\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: S&P downgraded Xerox Holdings to 'SD' (selective default) in Q1 2026 after repurchasing ~$101M face of 2028 senior unsecured notes at a discount (below-par = distressed exchange); Moody's downgraded Xerox to Caa2, negative outlook (and earlier to B2), citing worse-than-planned post-Lexmark performance; S&P had previously cut to CCC+ from B-; Xerox bonds lost ~half their value in 2025; 13.00% / 13.50% acquisition-financing coupons = capital raised at distressed pricing; Pro-forma gross debt/EBITDA ~6x at FY2025 year-end; Negative core free operating cash flow (~ -$170M to -$200M) excluding finance-receivable roll-off; Revenue declining 7-8% YoY in a secularly shrinking print/office-imaging market; Warrant/debt-for-equity exchange program flagged by Moody's as a potential distressed exchange; 2028 maturity wall with stated refinancing difficulty; Lexmark's own pre-acquisition debt was repaid at close (so the flagged Lexmark coupon is extinguished, not live)\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### BRC Group Holdings, Inc. (Other / unclassified, tier canary)\nTHESIS: The scan's headline ($1.10B gross debt, 16 deals, $583M near-term, 15 counterparties) is a ~30x data artifact and must be REJECTED. BRC Inc.'s actual total debt at FY2025 (Dec 2025) is ~$34.7M (current $2.4M + long-term $32.3M, net), down from ~$72M mid-2025 after an August 2025 $40.25M equity raise paid down debt. The $1.10B notional almost certainly double/triple-counts the firm's serial refinancings (2023 ~$50M term loan + $56M WhiteHawk ABL + up-to-$125M aggregate facility + 2024 ~$40M Blue Torch term loan + ABL revolver CAPACITY rather than drawn balance), likely also sweeping in operating-lease liabilities and/or the equity raise. ### Xerox Holdings Corporation, as holdings, Xerox Corporation (Other / unclassified, tier canary)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED already-distressed, but the scan's numbers are badly wrong and UNDERSTATE the danger. The 13.0% coupon flag is REAL and is NOT a penalty rate or artifact: Xerox issued $400M of 13.5% senior secured SECOND-LIEN notes due 2031 (alongside $400M of 10.25% first-lien notes due 2030) in April 2025 — genuine new-money distressed-priced issuance to fund the deal. ### BATTALION OIL CORP ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: REAL distress, but the XBRL flag is mismeasured and STALE — both directions matter. (1) The scan's gross debt (~$0.16B/$162M) is the PRE-refinance term loan; after the Fury Resources merger collapsed (Dec 20, 2024) Battalion drew a fresh, deeply-distressed $225M term loan (SOFR+7.75% +0.15% CSA ≈ ~12-13% all-in — a last-resort coupon the scan logged as 'n/a'), peaking at $225M, $213.8M at Q3'25, $208.1M at YE'25. ### ProFrac Holding Corp. ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED real distress, not an EBITDA-trough data artifact. ProFrac is a highly cyclical, capital-intensive pressure pumper hit by a two-year completions downturn (H1 2025 = fewest completed wells since 2021) plus weak oil prices, while carrying ~$1.05B of mostly secured floating-rate debt at penalty-grade spreads. The seed flag was correct in direction but used stale/trough inputs: actual FY2025 net debt is ~$1.03B (not $0.83B) and FY2025 Adjusted EBITDA fell to $310M from $501M in 2024 (-38%), with a quarterly trough of $41M in Q3 (10% margin). The seed's $0.19B EBITDA and 4.4x leverage appear to be a Q3-trough annualization; on a full-year basis net-debt/Adj.EBITDA is ~3.3x and Adj.EBITDA/interest ~2.2x. BUT the cash-flow reality is far tighter than headline leverage: FY2025 interest expense was $138.8M against operating cash flow of only $190M and free cash flow of just $25M (down from $185M in 2024), and Q3 2025 was FCF-negative (-$29M) with near-zero operating cash flow ($5M). Net loss widened to ~$356M in 2025. Liquidity is thin (~$152M total at YE2025, only ~$17M cash, rest ABL availability) and the company has repeatedly tapped the revolver, deferred Alpine term-loan amortization, and dribbled out incremental 2029 secured notes to plug 2025-2026 funding gaps. Both agencies acted: Moody's cut the CFR to Caa1 (notes Caa2, SGL-4 weakest liquidity) on 14-Jul-2025; S&P downgraded citing 'further liquidity deterioration or the possibility of a distressed transaction.' The fragility is genuine — a cyclical OFS name burning its liquidity cushion with secured debt and amortization pressure, where a continued slump or a single missed amortization/refinancing window forces a distressed exchange. Mitigants: no maturities until 2029, an in-progress ~$100M annualized cost-out program, Q4 2025 stabilization (Adj.EBITDA back to $61M, FCF +$14M), and a controlling family (Wilks) with incentive and means to support via related-party debt.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: high\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Moody's downgrade to Caa1 CFR / Caa2 notes / SGL-4 (weakest liquidity) on 14-Jul-2025, explicitly flagging distressed-exchange risk; S&P Global downgrade citing 'further liquidity deterioration or the possibility of ProFrac entering into a distressed transaction'; FY2025 net loss widened to ~$356M (from $208M in 2024); ~$374M per some compilations; FY2025 Adjusted EBITDA -38% YoY ($310M vs $501M); Q3 2025 trough $41M (10% margin); FY2025 FCF collapsed to $25M (from $185M); Q3 2025 FCF NEGATIVE -$29M with ~$5M operating cash flow; Thin liquidity: ~$16M cash + $66M ABL at Mar-2025, ~$17M cash + $135M ABL at Dec-2025; repeated revolver draws to fund operations; Three incremental $20M 2029 secured-note issuances in 2025 plus Alpine term-loan amortization deferral — classic liquidity-plugging behavior; ~$100M annualized cost-cut / labor-reduction program (layoffs) targeted by mid-2026; Equity collapse: analyst consensus 'Sell', price target ~$5.5, forecast unprofitable through 2026+; Penalty-grade Alpine term-loan margin of SOFR/Base + 7.25%\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### AMC ENTERTAINMENT HOLDINGS, INC. ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: Real but NOT acute/imminent distress — and meaningfully less extreme than the scan flagged. AMC's core fragility is structural, not a near-term wall: it cannot service its debt from operations. FY2025 Adjusted EBITDA was ~$387.5M against ~$450M annual interest expense (cash interest coverage ~0.86x) and free cash flow was -$365.9M; net loss was -$632.4M. The gap is plugged not by operations but by a perpetual cycle of (a) serial equity dilution (shareholders doubled authorized shares to 1.1B in 2025; ~7% projected annual dilution; debt-for-equity exchanges) and (b) liability-management refinancings that trade lower principal for time. Through 2024-2026 AMC pushed essentially ALL 2026 and 2027 maturities (incl. 5.875% subs, 10/12% PIK 2nd-lien, and the 12.75% Odeon notes) out to 2029-2031 — confirming the scan's '~$0.00B near-term' finding. So the canary does NOT trip on a maturity wall in 2026-2027; it trips on the day capital markets stop funding the dilution/refi machine (equity price collapse closing the ATM, or credit markets repricing). ### Wheels Up Experience Inc. ((XBRL-negEBITDA), tier neg_ebitda_levered)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED structurally fragile, not a one-off loss year and not early-stage-with-cash. Wheels Up has incinerated cash for years: GAAP net loss -$339.6M (FY24), -$294.2M (FY25), -$82.9M (Q1'26); FY25 net cash USED in operations was -$166.3M; stockholders' equity is NEGATIVE -$392.1M. Despite a real operating turnaround (first positive Adj. EBITDA in Q4'25 at +$32.9M), full-year FY25 Adj. EBITDA was still -$43.5M and the company reverted to -$28.1M Adj. EBITDA in Q1'26 (seasonality), confirming the seed's negative-EBITDA / levered flag. The acute fragility is liquidity, not the maturity wall: cash collapsed from $133.9M (12/31/25) to $54.1M (3/31/26) — ~$80M burned in one quarter — forcing a fresh $100M Delta-led 12% PIK term loan announced/closed May 2026. The deferred-revenue model is the hidden run-risk: ~$687.6M (Q1'26) of member prepaid flight blocks is a negative-working-capital float that vastly exceeds the $54.1M cash balance; it has funded operations but reverses violently if membership confidence erodes (and the stock has twice gone sub-$1 / faces NYSE delisting, reverse-split planned). The single reason this is HIGH rather than already-distressed: it is a captive, repeatedly-recapitalized affiliate — Delta (~36% holder, largest shareholder), Cox and CK Wheels have serially injected rescue capital (2023 ~$500M / ~95% stake; 2025 $100M; 2026 $100M + $165M aircraft facility) and extended lock-ups, functioning as a de facto sponsor backstop. ### FMC CORP ((XBRL-negEBITDA), tier neg_ebitda_levered)\nTHESIS: The scan's \"negative EBITDA + levered = cash-burning early-stage burner\" framing is WRONG on mechanism but RIGHT that the entity is fragile — for a completely different reason. The $-1.24B EBITDA is a GAAP artifact: FY2025 carried a $1.356B non-cash goodwill impairment (write-off of the ENTIRE goodwill balance, triggered by the stock-price collapse). Adjusted EBITDA was solidly POSITIVE at ~$843M in 2025. So this is not a cash-burning startup; it is a ~140-year-old industrial with real operating profit. The REAL fragility is a structural earnings cliff: FMC's core Rynaxypyr/chlorantraniliprole composition-of-matter patent has expired country-by-country (Aug 2022 onward, rolling through ~2027), exposing 30-40% of revenue to generic competition in Asia/LatAm. The result is a self-reinforcing downgrade spiral: (1) FY2025 revenue fell 18% to $3.47B; (2) 2026 guidance projects Adjusted EBITDA DOWN another ~17% to $670-730M and FCF of -$65M to +$65M (breakeven at midpoint, after a NEGATIVE -$165M FCF in 2025); (3) S&P cut FMC to BB+ (fallen angel into junk) in Nov 2025 with negative outlook; (4) net leverage is ~3.9x (net debt ~$3.49B / $843M adj. EBITDA) and bank-defined TOTAL leverage hit 5.67x at Q1'26; (5) the company had to AMEND its revolver — the maximum total-leverage covenant was effectively waived/not-measured for Q1'26 and won't be tested again until Dec 31 2026, when it is RELAXED to 6.75x (from 6.0x); (6) FMC pledged a ~$6-9B collateral package and issued $750M of FIRST-LIEN SENIOR SECURED notes due 2031 — a structural subordination event for a former IG name that historically issued unsecured paper; (7) dividend cut by ~$250M/yr (Q3'25), buybacks halted to Q4'27; (8) in early 2026 the Board authorized exploration of strategic options \"including but not limited to the sale of the company.\" This is a credit in genuine, management-acknowledged distress — but it is a deleveraging/franchise-erosion distress with positive cash EBITDA, NOT a runway-to-zero burn. Severity is high but NOT already-distressed: it is still EBITDA-positive, has covenant relief secured, and the 2026 maturity wall is being termed out via the secured notes.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: high\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: S&P downgrade to BB+ (junk / fallen angel) Nov 2025 with NEGATIVE outlook, citing patent cliff and weak credit metrics; S&P cut its 2026 EBITDA forecast to $850M from $959M; $1.356B goodwill impairment in FY2025 — full write-off of goodwill, triggered by stock-price collapse (this is the source of the -$1.24B GAAP EBITDA flag); FY2025 GAAP net loss of $2.24B ($17.88/sh) vs $341.6M profit in 2024; Revolver covenant AMENDED: max total-leverage covenant effectively waived/not-measured for Q1'26 and relaxed to 6.75x (from 6.0x) for the Dec'26-Dec'27 window — actual 5.67x at Q1'26; Structural subordination: shifted from unsecured to SECURED debt — $750M first-lien notes due 2031 + revolver now secured by ~$6-9B collateral package; former investment-grade issuer pledging substantially all assets; Dividend cut by ~$250M/year (Q3'25); share buybacks suspended until Q4'27; Board authorized exploration of strategic options 'including but not limited to the sale of the company' (early 2026) — a go-private/break-up signal; Negative free cash flow -$165M in 2025 (down $779M YoY); 2026 guided to roughly breakeven; Securities class-action litigation filed (E.D. Pa.) relating to prior channel-inventory/destocking disclosures; India commercial business divestiture (held-for-sale ~$611.7M) — shrinking the revenue base; 2% headwind to 2026\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### ChargePoint Holdings, Inc. ((XBRL-negEBITDA), tier neg_ebitda_levered)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED fragile, and the original seed UNDERSTATES it. This is not an early-stage-with-cash company nor a one-off loss year: ChargePoint has incurred net operating losses and negative operating cash flow EVERY year since inception (net losses of ~$457.6M / $277.1M / $220.2M over the last three FYs), accumulated deficit $2.15B, and as of Apr 30, 2026 has crossed into NEGATIVE stockholders' equity of $(9.1M). The seed's debt picture is stale: after the Nov 14, 2025 distressed exchange, the capital structure is now a $156.5M principal SENIOR SECURED term loan at a 12% fixed coupon due Jan 31, 2030 (first-priority lien on substantially all assets incl. IP), plus only ~$11.8M residual 2028 converts. The seed's 'max coupon n/a' and 'near-term debt ~$0' are both misleading: the real coupon is a punitive 12% (a clear distressed-financing print — the prior 2028 notes were exchanged at a >50% haircut with $25M cash + warrants struck at $25 vs a sub-$10 stock), and while there is no 2026–27 maturity wall, the binding constraint is a hard $25.0M MINIMUM-LIQUIDITY COVENANT TESTED MONTHLY. The fragility is liquidity/covenant-driven, not maturity-driven. With $95.8M cash at Apr 30, 2026, ~$36.6M quarterly operating burn (cash fell $45.8M in the quarter), and the $25M floor, usable cushion is ~$70.8M = roughly 2–3 quarters of burn before the covenant trips, absent further cost cuts or a dilutive raise. The PIK toggle (interest payable in stock until Nov 14, 2026, subject to a 19.99% dilution cap) defers ~$18.8M/yr of cash interest only temporarily — cash interest is mandatory after Nov 2026, accelerating the burn just as the runway tightens. Mitigants: management asserts >12 months of runway, the company is undrawn on no revolver (the $150M revolver was TERMINATED Nov 14, 2025), the term loan is non-amortizing (only $15.6M current), and adjusted-EBITDA loss is narrowing (H2 FY26 down 29%). But the equity is already worthless on a book basis, NYSE compliance required a 1-for-20 reverse split (Jul 2025), and the term loan is now structurally senior to equity with a lien on the IP. Net read: a genuinely distressed, cash-burning, now-balance-sheet-insolvent levered name whose first failure point is a monthly liquidity-covenant breach rather than a refinancing wall.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: high\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Distressed debt exchange Nov 2025: $328.6M of 2028 notes exchanged at >50% face reduction for $186.5M new senior secured loan + $25M cash + warrants struck at $25 vs sub-$10 stock; 12% fixed coupon on the new senior secured term loan — punitive, distressed-financing pricing; NEGATIVE stockholders' equity $(9.1M) as of Apr 30 2026 (balance-sheet insolvent); accumulated deficit $2.15B; 1-for-20 reverse stock split effective Jul 28 2025 to avoid NYSE delisting; March 2026 reorganization cut ~10% of global workforce (146 employees), $8.4M restructuring charge; Cash fell $45.8M in a single quarter (Jan 31 -> Apr 30 2026) to $95.8M; operating burn $36.6M/quarter; New $25.0M minimum-liquidity covenant tested MONTHLY — a near-term breach trigger against depleting cash; $150M revolving credit facility terminated Nov 14 2025 (loss of backstop liquidity); Net operating losses and negative operating cash flow in EVERY year since inception, expected to continue; Revenue declining (FY26 -1% YoY; some quarters -9% to -15.7% YoY); analysts UBS/Goldman neutral/sell; Management runway statement explicitly hedged: 'may be required to seek additional equity or debt financing'\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### Babcock & Wilcox Enterprises, Inc. (Other / unclassified, tier canary)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED canary — but the fragility is NOT the balance sheet the scan flagged; it is contract/backlog quality plus a related-party circular-financing web. The scan's gross-debt figure (~$0.76B) is stale (FY2024 vintage). FY2025 actuals: $321.1M total debt, $119.7M net debt, falling to $42.4M net debt by Q1 2026 after the May-2025 exchange (~$132M of 2026 notes swapped into $100.8M of 8.75% second-lien notes due 2030) and ongoing bond repurchases — so the leverage(0.12)/refi(0.03) signals are LOW and largely self-cured. The genuinely load-bearing fragility is that ~85% of the $2.824B \"pro forma backlog\" (and essentially the entire 4,400% YoY equity move) is ONE contract: the $2.4B \"Base Electron\" AI-data-center power project. Wolfpack Research (Mar 12, 2026) alleges only ~$434M of that $2.4B is a fixed fee (rest contingent/variable), and that Applied Digital's guarantee of Base Electron dissolves if Base Electron raises just $50M of financing or goes public. The concentration(HHI 1.00) and circular(0.30)/related-party signals are ECONOMICALLY REAL, not data artifacts: Base Electron is an SPV spun out of Applied Digital's team whose registered address matches the HQ of BRC Group (formerly B. Riley Financial) — B&W's LARGEST SHAREHOLDER and a related-party LENDER (the second-lien notes share collateral with the B. Riley junior secured note). Bryant Riley sits on both the B&W cap table and Base Electron's board, and allegedly sold stock (~$10.4M) into the spike. Multiple securities-fraud class actions (Pomerantz; Levi & Korsinsky) allege the contract value was inflated and that BRC was provided \"exit liquidity.\" Underlying cash generation is weak: FY2025 operating cash flow was NEGATIVE $68.9M while the company still paid $14.9M of preferred dividends; adjusted EBITDA $43.7M barely exceeds $37.5M interest expense (~1.1x, and only after heavy add-backs). The canary signature: an obscure, chronically cash-burning small-cap whose survival narrative and equity value rest on a single, contingent, related-party-tangled contract — it re-rates (and de-rates) on contract credibility, not on operations.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: high\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Stale-vs-actual: scan's ~$0.76B gross debt is FY2024-vintage; actual FY2025 total debt $321.1M — a downward-trending, not exploding, balance sheet; FY2025 operating cash flow NEGATIVE $68.9M while still paying $14.9M preferred dividends (cash burn masked by equity raises); Distressed-style May-2025 liability management: ~$131.8M of 2026 notes exchanged for $100.8M of new 8.75% second-lien 2030 notes (~24% face reduction = sub-par exchange); 10-K retains 'substantial doubt / going concern' RISK-FACTOR language tied to the Dec-2026 note refinancing obligation (forward-looking, not a current going-concern qualification); Equity up ~4,400% YoY on a single contract — classic small-cap narrative blow-off; -11.6% on Mar-12-2026 Wolfpack report; Multiple securities-fraud class actions (Pomerantz, Levi & Korsinsky) alleging inflated $2.4B contract value and concealed related-party ties; Related-party / circular financing: largest shareholder & lender BRC (ex-B. ### X.AI Corp, as holdings, X.AI LLC (Data center / AI infra, tier beam)\nTHESIS: The seed flag (\"~$20B gross debt, ~$20B due <=2027, max coupon 2.0%, front-loaded refi wall, 1 non-recourse SPV\") is a DATA ARTIFACT on the headline numbers but points at a REAL, filing-confirmed fragility that has been transferred up to the parent. Corrections: (1) The 2.0% coupon is wrong — xAI's actual paper was a $3.0B tranche of 12.50% Senior Secured Notes due June 2030 plus a 12.5%-area Fixed Rate Loan and a SOFR+725bp Floating Rate Loan (the ~12.5% level is textbook distressed-borrower pricing). The ~2-4.58% the scanner likely latched onto is the SpaceX-level refinanced bridge, not xAI. (2) The '$20B due <=2027' refi wall no longer sits at xAI: on March 5, 2026 ALL of xAI's funded debt (Fixed Rate Loan, Floating Rate Loan, $3B 12.5% Notes) plus X's two term loans were extinguished and rolled into the $20,000M unsecured SpaceX Bridge Loan (March 2026; matures Sep 2, 2027, two 3-mo extensions to Mar 2028) at the SPaceX level, triggering a $1,526M loss on extinguishment and a $518M prepayment penalty. The bridge must be repaid within 6 months of SpaceX's IPO (which opened June 12, 2026), so the refi wall is real but is now SpaceX's, with X.AI LLC as a joint-and-several guarantor. (3) The genuine hidden-leverage / ring-fenced signature is REAL and filing-confirmed: the Valor Equity Partners GPU equipment leases — three agreements aggregating $20.2B of cash payments ($6,986M + $6,633M + $6,587M) where an xAI subsidiary (CTC) leases Nvidia GPUs from Valor entities (controlled by SpaceX director Antonio Gracias). PwC ruled these 'failed sale-leasebacks,' forcing ~$9.9B of related debt onto SpaceX's balance sheet ($455M current + $4,052M + $5,365M). This is related-party, GPU-collateralized, SpaceX-guaranteed financing that behaves like secured debt, not operating leases. NET READ: xAI standalone is deeply cash-negative (FY2025: ~$6.4B operating loss on ~$3.2B revenue; Q3 widening to ~$1.46B net loss/qtr; ~$1B/mo burn; ~$0.5B ARR vs ~$30B annualized capex run-rate) and could not service 12.5% debt on its own cash flows — it was a distressed credit. But it is NO LONGER a standalone refi-wall canary: the fragility was absorbed by SpaceX, which de-risked the coupon (12.5% -> ~4.58% bridge) and now carries the consolidated obligation and the Valor failed-SLB debt. ### NEWELL BRANDS INC. ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: REAL but MODERATE distress — the seed flag is materially overstated by a data artifact. So this is NOT a 1.2x-coverage near-default canary; it is a structurally levered, low-growth consumer-staples turnaround whose true fragility is: (1) secular volume decline in discretionary categories, (2) a one-way ratchet UP in cost of capital — the 4.200% 2026 notes were refinanced in May 2025 into 8.50% 2028 notes, roughly doubling that tranche's coupon, (3) tariff exposure ($174M cash cost in 2025, ~$0.07 EPS headwind guided into 2026) compressing already-thin margins, and (4) twin 2025 rating downgrades to single-B/B+ junk with negative-to-stable outlooks. ### CENTURY CASINOS INC /CO/ ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: CONFIRMED fragile, but the XBRL flag had the RIGHT conclusion for the WRONG reason. The scan's \"2.6x net-debt/EBITDA, $0.33B debt, 1.09x coverage\" is a DATA ARTIFACT: it captures only the $332.5M Goldman term loan and excludes the ~$712M VICI Master Lease long-term financing obligation (a sale-leaseback recorded as financing debt under ASC 842 failed-sale treatment) and the ~$67M/yr of Master Lease financing 'interest' (~$16.9M in Q1 2026 alone). On a lease-inclusive basis the real picture is far worse: management-reported net debt/EBITDA 6.9x and lease-adjusted 7.6x at YE2025. The genuine fragility is a FIXED-CHARGE / RENT-COVERAGE squeeze, not a refinancing wall. Adjusted EBITDAR of $105.4M (FY2025, +3% YoY) must cover ~$66.4M cash rent (2025), rising to ~$75.2M guided for 2026, PLUS ~$32M cash term-loan interest (6.00% margin over SOFR on $332.5M) — fixed charges consume the bulk of EBITDAR, leaving the GAAP entity deeply loss-making ($61.4M net loss FY2025; $16.5M net loss Q1 2026) and burning cash ($69.0M → $60.0M cash in one quarter). The triple-net rent escalates and cannot be deleveraged by paydown; it is a permanent senior claim on every dollar of property cash flow. MITIGANTS that argue against imminent failure: (1) NO debt maturities until 2029 (term loan April 1 2029; revolver April 1 2027 but undrawn); (2) the 5.50x springing First-Lien Net Leverage covenant was EXCEEDED at 3/31/2026 but is NOT in effect because zero revolver/LC drawn (springs only above $10.5M revolver usage) — so no covenant breach, no default; (3) EBITDAR is GROWING (+24% Q1 2026 YoY, +13% Q4 2025) and capex is falling (€18M → €14-15M), so the cash bleed should narrow; (4) the lease is an operating obligation that cannot be 'accelerated' like a bond. Net read: a structurally over-levered, persistently GAAP-unprofitable equity story with thin fixed-charge cushion and ongoing cash erosion, but NOT an imminent default/restructuring canary — runway is multi-year barring a property-cash-flow shock. Severity = high on leverage/coverage, but the binary-default risk is low until the 2029 wall or a revolver draw.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: high\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Persistent large GAAP net losses: $(61.4)M FY2025 (loss widened ~60% vs prior year on the change measure), $(16.5)M Q1 2026, $(20.6)M Q1 2025 — chronic bottom-line losses; Cash declined $69.0M → $60.0M in one quarter (Q1 2026), ~$9M burn; Management-reported leverage 6.9x net debt/EBITDA, 7.6x lease-adjusted — well into distressed territory for a regional gaming operator; First Lien Net Leverage Ratio EXCEEDED the 5.50x springing covenant threshold at 3/31/2026 (covenant only avoided because revolver is undrawn — a thin technical reason, signals balance-sheet stress); Escalating triple-net rent: cash rent rising $66.4M (2025) → ~$75.2M (2026 guidance), a growing senior fixed claim ahead of any EBITDAR growth; Operating headwinds in 2025: loss of Colorado sports-betting income and Poland (Casinos Poland) licensing disruptions pressured EBITDAR; Stated capital-allocation pivot to debt paydown over buybacks — management acknowledging the leverage problem; High-cost term debt: SOFR + 600bps margin signals the credit market prices CNTY as a stressed/levered issuer\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### Huntsman CORP ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: REAL but CYCLICAL-TROUGH distress, NOT a clean data artifact and NOT imminent default. The XBRL flag (ND/EBITDA 10.8x, coverage 0.63x) is half-true: it uses a GAAP/trailing EBITDA denominator (~$0.16B) that is depressed by restructuring and impairment charges. On management's adjusted basis, FY2025 EBITDA was $275M, putting net debt/EBITDA at ~5.8x (still elevated, junk territory) and interest coverage near ~1.8-2.3x — meaningfully above the 0.63x the screen shows. Independent corroboration that this is genuine stress, not noise: (1) S&P downgraded HUN to junk (BB+ from BBB-) in 2025 on a sustained EBITDA-margin collapse — the third consecutive year of underperformance; (2) the company negotiated a Second Amendment to its 2022 revolver (May 23, 2025) to RAISE the maximum permitted Consolidated Net Debt/EBITDA covenant through Dec 31, 2026 (a 'Covenant Relief Period') and simultaneously tightened debt/lien baskets and restricted upstreaming of cash to the parent — a textbook covenant-pressure signal; (3) the board cut the dividend 65% (to $0.0875/qtr) to preserve cash; (4) FY2025 net loss of $284M and Q4 adjusted EBITDA collapsed to just $35M ($96M Polyurethanes loss), so the run-rate is still deteriorating, not recovering. The offsetting reality that keeps this out of acute-distress: ~$1.3B liquidity (revolver + $429M cash + securitization), FY2025 free cash flow still positive at $125M, no debt maturity until the $700M 4.60% notes due 2027, and the CFO publicly stating no 2026 covenant concern. Net read: a leveraged, junk-rated, demand-starved cyclical at a margin trough where the screen's 10.8x/0.63x overstates severity by using trough GAAP EBITDA; the binding risk is a prolonged MDI/construction downturn that exhausts covenant headroom before the cycle turns, plus a 2027 refinancing wall, not a near-term liquidity failure.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: moderate\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: S&P downgrade to junk: BB+ from BBB- (2025), third consecutive year of EBITDA-margin underperformance; issue-level notes also cut to BB+; Credit-agreement Second Amendment (May 23, 2025) RAISING the max Net Debt/EBITDA covenant through Dec 31, 2026 ('Covenant Relief Period') — explicit covenant-headroom pressure; Same amendment tightened general debt/lien baskets and restricted Huntsman International from upstreaming cash to the parent for equity redemptions — lender control tightening; Dividend cut 65% (to $0.0875/qtr, $0.35/yr) to preserve cash; FY2025 net loss of $284M (worse than 2024's $189M loss); Q4 2025 net loss $96M; Adjusted EBITDA down for a third year: $414M (2024) to $275M (2025); Q4 2025 collapsed to $35M; $100M+ restructuring: facility closures, exit of Akron nitrile-latex business by Q1 2027, 25-30% workforce reduction in that line; Q4 2025 Polyurethanes segment loss of ~$96M on weak MDI margins / Asia oversupply; Multiple sell-side downgrades (Wells Fargo, BofA) on tariff pressure and demand weakness\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? Is the high coupon a one-off / penalty / mis-extraction? Already refinanced or covenant-cured? Is the leverage matched by hard assets or stable contracted revenue? Is the flagged signal a data artifact? ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: The XBRL flag (net-debt/EBITDA 22.8x, interest coverage 0.62x, net debt $1.84B) is LARGELY A DATA ARTIFACT typical of a non-bank mortgage originator, but it overlays a genuine, smaller corporate-credit fragility. (3) The scan's \"near-term (≤2027) ~$0.00B\" is WRONG: there IS a 2027 wall (the 8.750% Senior Secured Notes, ~$340.6M, mature Nov 2027) and loanDepot just CASH-repaid its residual 2025 unsecured notes in Q4 2025 (cash dropped $122M to $337M partly for that repayment) — so the maturity ladder is real, not zero. The genuine residual fragility: this is a structurally loss-making (-$108M FY2025 GAAP net loss, 4th consecutive annual loss), CCC/B-tier-quality non-bank originator with a thin and DECLINING cash buffer ($459M Q3 → $337M Q4 2025 → ~$277M unrestricted by Q1 2026), facing a ~$340M secured 2027 maturity plus a ~$498M unsecured 2028 maturity (6.125% notes) that must be refinanced in a high-rate environment where its prior refi required cash sweeteners and step-up coupons (6.5%→8.75%). ### HARROW, INC. ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: REJECT the distress flag as a stale, trough-window artifact. The XBRL seed (net-debt/EBITDA 6.5x, interest coverage 1.49x, EBITDA ~$0.03B) was computed off a TTM window that straddled (a) a high-cost legacy capital stack — an Oaktree term loan plus two retail 'baby bond' tranches at 8.625% (HROWL, due 2026) and a penalty-grade 11.875% (HROWM, due 2027) — and (b) a depressed EBITDA base during commercial ramp. Both legs reversed in 2025. In Sept 2025 Harrow issued $250M of 8.625% senior unsecured notes due 2030 (later upsized via a $50M add-on) and used proceeds to fully repay the $107.5M Oaktree facility, redeem the $75M HROWL 2026 notes and $40.25M HROWM 2027 notes, AND added a new $40M Fifth Third revolver. Result: NO near-term maturity wall — first maturity is 2030. Simultaneously, adjusted EBITDA rose from $40.3M (2024) to $61.9M (2025), with FY2026 guidance of $80–100M; operating cash flow swung from -$22.2M (2024) to +$43.9M (2025); GAAP net loss narrowed to $5.1M. The 1.49x coverage and 6.5x leverage readings are genuine transitional-year prints but reflect a legacy cost structure and an EBITDA trough that no longer exist. On run-rate FY2026, net leverage is ~2.0–2.5x and interest coverage clears 1.5x comfortably with growing headroom. Residual risk is operational concentration (two products = bulk of revenue), not balance-sheet fragility.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: low\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: FY2025 still GAAP net loss (-$5.1M), though sharply narrowed — not yet consistently GAAP-profitable; Legacy reliance on retail 'baby bond' financing at up to 11.875% indicated prior funding-cost stress (now refinanced away); Transitional-year interest coverage ~1.1–1.5x (the basis of the XBRL flag) — real but trough/legacy-driven; Single-product reimbursement exposure: IHEEZO depends on favorable Medicare J-code / pass-through reimbursement; an adverse coverage change would pressure ~a third of revenue; Two products ≈ half of revenue — operational, not credit, concentration\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? Is the high coupon a one-off / penalty / mis-extraction? Already refinanced or covenant-cured? Is the leverage matched by hard assets or stable contracted revenue? Is the flagged signal a data artifact? ### AIR T INC ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: PARTIALLY CONFIRMED, but mechanism is mis-stated by the seed. The XBRL 25x net-debt/EBITDA and 0.75x coverage off the March-2025 10-K are directionally REAL (Adj. EBITDA ~$7.4M vs interest expense ~$8.4M = ~0.88x EBITDA coverage; GAAP operating result is a loss), BUT they reflect a chronically thin-margin, highly-financialized holding company, NOT an imminent liquidity crisis: FY2025 operating cash flow was a STRONGLY POSITIVE ~$23.5M and working capital was +$30.9M, so the firm self-funds interest via working-capital release/trading inventory, not earnings. The genuine, FRESHER fragility is the FY2026 trajectory: (1) consolidated debt nearly doubled from ~$129M (Mar-2025) to ~$194-212M (Dec-2025), driven by the Rex regional-airline acquisition which is itself loss-making (Rex net loss ~$1.5M in its stub period); (2) Q3 FY2026 (Dec-2025) revenue fell 9% YoY to $71.1M with a $3.8M operating LOSS and Adj. EBITDA collapsing to ~$0.2M; (3) working capital flipped to a deficit (~-$6.6M at Dec-2025); (4) common-equity is around breakeven-to-negative (total equity ~$5.5M is propped by redeemable noncontrolling interests). Concentration is the binary tail: FedEx is ~39% of consolidated revenue and the FedEx dry-lease/feeder agreements expire Aug 31, 2026 with 90-day termination rights — a near-term cliff the \"≤2027 maturities ~$0.00B\" scan field entirely misses. Net: a fragile, over-levered micro-cap that is NOT distressed-today on cash flow, but is on a deteriorating earnings path with rising leverage, negative working capital, a customer cliff, and thin equity cushion. ### Caesars Entertainment, Inc. ((XBRL-distressed), tier xbrl_distress)\nTHESIS: The XBRL flag is REAL but the framing understates the true fixed-charge burden while overstating acute default risk. Two corrections: (1) the $11.9B \"gross debt\" is ONLY the funded stack — Caesars also pays ~$1.1B/yr of senior, fixed, economically-debt-like master-lease rent (VICI regional ~$730M + Las Vegas/Forum + GLPI), capitalized as financing obligations of roughly $11-12B; this is why one aggregator showed ~$24.9B total \"debt.\" Including leases, true rent-adjusted leverage is ~6-7x, not 3x. (2) On the funded stack alone the entity is leveraged but currently SERVICEABLE: FY2025 net debt $11.018B / Adj. EBITDA $3.624B = 3.04x (not the seed 3.4x — the seed used a trough EBITDA of $3.27B); EBITDA/net-interest coverage = $3.624B/$2.304B = 1.57x (not the seed 1.41x). The $502M FY2025 net loss is an interest-and-D&A artifact, NOT operating distress — EBITDA is positive and rising, with Caesars Digital newly profitable ($85M Q4 EBITDA vs $20M). The genuine, mispriced fragility is the master-lease overhang colliding with thin coverage: the VICI regional master lease pays ~$730M rent against ~$750M of leased-property cash flow (~1.03x property-level coverage), and VICI is PUBLICLY discussing a rent cut in exchange for Caesars capital commitments — a direct tell that the regional leased pool is stressed at the asset level even though the parent covenant is clean. Net: not an imminent canary, but a structurally over-leveraged, lease-encumbered operator whose equity (D/E ~295%) and unsecured notes are the thin tranche if regional gaming demand softens or rates stay high into the 2028-2030 maturity wall.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: moderate\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: FY2025 GAAP net loss $502M (widened from $278M loss in 2024) — driven by $2.3B net interest + D&A, not operating decline; Interest coverage thin at 1.57x (EBITDA/net-interest); below a comfortable cushion; VICI publicly discussing a RENT REDUCTION on the Caesars Regional Master Lease in exchange for Caesars capital commitments — landlord-side stress signal; regional leased pool coverage ~1.03x (rent $730M vs $750M property cash flow); Rent-adjusted leverage ~6-7x once ~$11-12B master-lease financing obligations are included — funded-only 3x metric materially understates fixed-charge burden; High D/E ~295%; equity is the thin tranche; Capital allocation tension: buying back stock ($420M since mid-2024) while still carrying ~$11.9B debt + lease overhang\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### WOLFSPEED, INC. ((XBRL-negEBITDA), tier neg_ebitda_levered)\nTHESIS: PARTIALLY REJECT the scan's forward-canary framing — the distress event ALREADY happened and is now in the rear-view. Wolfspeed filed pre-packaged Chapter 11 in July 2025 and emerged Sept 29-30, 2025, cutting ~$4.6B of debt (~70%), wiping out legacy equity (old holders left with ~3-5%), and converting Renesas's $2.06B prepayment deposit into equity + a $204M convertible. The scan's snapshot (neg EBITDA $-1.08B, net debt $1.03B, gross debt $1.72B) is a real but STALE read: gross debt ~$1.72B is now the POST-restructuring stack (LT debt $922.2M New Senior Secured Notes due 2030 + convertible notes net $798.3M due 2031), and near-term (<=2027) maturities are genuinely ~$0 — everything is termed out to 2030/2031. Going-concern doubt that appeared in early/mid-2025 filings has been REMOVED: the Q3 FY2026 10-Q (period ended 3/29/2026) is explicitly prepared on a going-concern basis with no substantial-doubt qualifier, backed by $1.16B cash+ST investments. So this is NOT a pre-default fragility find. The RESIDUAL fragility that remains is genuine but second-order: (1) the company is still deeply cash-burning — Q3 FY2026 operating cash flow ~($84M), adjusted EBITDA ($62M), GAAP gross margin (27)%, net loss ($120M) — at ~$150M/qtr revenue against a still-soft EV/SiC demand backdrop; (2) the New Senior Secured Notes carry a PUNITIVE coupon (currently 9.875% cash + 4.00%, stepping after 6/23/2026 to 13.875% only if a hard step-down condition is met, else 15.875% cash), and the step-down requires paying notes below $1B AND receiving >=$450M of CHIPS grant disbursements — neither yet achieved; (3) deleveraging hinges on the still-undisbursed $750M CHIPS direct grant (only the separate advanced-manufacturing tax credit refunds of ~$698.6M have actually landed) plus a demand recovery. Net: a reorganized, liquidity-cushioned but loss-making turnaround whose runway is ~3-4 years of cash at the current burn against no near-term wall — fragile as a credit (high coupon, negative FCF, execution-dependent) but NOT an imminent default canary. The XBRL neg-EBITDA flag is a true-positive on cash burn and a partial false-positive on imminent distress (the distress already cleared via Chapter 11).\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: moderate\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Pre-packaged Chapter 11 filed July 2025; emerged Sept 29-30, 2025 — distress event ALREADY realized; Legacy common equity effectively wiped out (~70% debt cut, reorg equity to creditors/Renesas; old holders left with low-single-digit %); Reverse-split-equivalent share collapse: weighted basic shares fell from ~154M (Predecessor) to ~39M (Successor) and EPS ($3.05)/qtr loss; Going-concern substantial-doubt language present in PRE-emergence FY2025/early-FY2026 filings (now REMOVED in the post-emergence Q3 FY2026 10-Q); Continuing deep operating losses post-emergence: Q3 FY2026 net loss ($119.9M), operating loss ($114.3M), adjusted EBITDA ($62M), GAAP gross margin (27)%; Negative operating cash flow ($84M)/qtr; ($126.4M) over the 6-month Successor stub; Punitive new-money coupon: 1L notes step to 13.875%-15.875% cash after June 23, 2026 unless CHIPS-linked step-down condition is met; Demand headwind explicitly disclosed: 'current softening demand' and 'competitive industry' cited in the going-concern assessment; EV slowdown in US/EU; $750M CHIPS direct grant still undisbursed as of Q3 FY2026 (only ~$698.6M of separate tax-credit refunds received)\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? Is the high coupon a one-off / penalty / mis-extraction? Already refinanced or covenant-cured? Is the leverage matched by hard assets or stable contracted revenue? Is the flagged signal a data artifact? ### Six Flags Entertainment Corporation/NEW ((XBRL-negEBITDA), tier neg_ebitda_levered)\nTHESIS: REJECT the negative-EBITDA flag as a DATA ARTIFACT, but retain a MODERATE leverage concern. The XBRL \"-$0.89B EBITDA\" is the GAAP net/operating-loss line distorted by a ~$1.5B NON-CASH goodwill & intangible impairment booked in FY2025; it is NOT cash burn. On a cash basis the business generated +$792M Adjusted EBITDA on $3.10B revenue in FY2025. So \"cash-burning AND levered\" is wrong on the first leg — this is a positive-EBITDA, asset-heavy, established operator, not an early-stage burner. The REAL fragility is a leveraged-recap/integration story: net debt ~$5.11B against $792M EBITDA = ~6.45x net leverage, well above management's ~4.0x target and S&P's 4.5x downgrade threshold. Post-merger EBITDA badly undershot underwriting (initial $1.08–1.12B guidance cut ~30% to $780–805M actual) on falling attendance (FY25 attendance -? ; Q4 attendance -13%) and higher costs, triggering a two-notch Moody's CFR downgrade to B2 (from Ba3, Nov 2025) and an S&P negative outlook. FCF was negative LTM (~-$89M) under heavy CapEx (~$475M in 2025), and the dividend was suspended. HOWEVER, near-term maturity wall is largely defused: the $1.0B of April-2027 notes (5.375%/5.500%) were refinanced in Jan 2026 via $1.0B of 8.625% notes due 2032, pushing the next maturity to the 2028 notes ($300M 6.50%); 2026 interest guided to only $135–145M (covered ~5–6x by EBITDA); liquidity ~$623M; CapEx cut to $400–425M for 2026; ~$331M of asset sales (7 parks) to deleverage; all excess FCF directed to debt paydown. Net: a fragile-but-not-failing high-yield credit — interest is well-covered and there is no imminent maturity or going-concern issue, but it is a B2 cyclical, attendance-sensitive name whose deleveraging path depends on a consumer/attendance recovery, and a recession that depresses gate attendance is the asymmetric risk. Severity is MODERATE, not high/already-distressed.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: moderate\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Moody's two-notch downgrade to B2 CFR from Ba3 (Nov 17, 2025) citing post-merger credit metrics 'substantially worse' than underwriting; S&P outlook revised to negative on delayed debt reduction; adjusted leverage expected above 4.5x downgrade trigger through 2025–2026; FY2025 Adjusted EBITDA cut ~30% vs initial guidance ($1.08–1.12B initial -> $780–805M actual, $792M reported); $1.5B non-cash goodwill/intangible impairment in FY2025 (drove $1.60B net loss) — the source of the negative-EBITDA XBRL flag; Dividend suspended post-merger (no dividend currently paid); Negative LTM free cash flow (~-$89M) under heavy CapEx; Attendance weakness: Q4 2025 attendance -13% YoY; revenue -5% YoY in Q4; Net leverage ~6.45x vs ~4.0x management target — deleveraging behind plan; Asset sales: agreement to sell 7 regional parks for ~$331M (portfolio optimization / deleveraging)\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ### EchoStar CORP ((XBRL-negEBITDA), tier neg_ebitda_levered)\nTHESIS: The -$16.14B \"negative EBITDA\" flag is a DATA ARTIFACT, not operating burn. It is driven by a ~$16.5B non-cash impairment recorded in Q3 2025 (nine-month impairments/other charges of $16.48B; Q3 operating loss of ~$16.6B), taken to write down the 5G network buildout and spectrum carrying value when the AT&T/SpaceX asset-sale agreements reset fair value. The underlying operating businesses generate POSITIVE segment OIBDA (Q2 2025: ~$280M wireless OIBDA, $663M Pay-TV OIBDA on $3.7B revenue), and management guided to positive operating free cash flow for FY2025. So the seed hypothesis of \"$-16.14B cash-burning\" is REJECTED as a literal cash-flow read. HOWEVER, a genuine and separate fragility exists on a LIQUIDITY-TIMING basis: ~$24.1-24.6B of principal debt against only ~$1.5B cash (Q1 2026), with a heavy 2026 maturity wall (~$6.2B due within 12 months at Q1 2026: $2.0B 7.75% notes, ~$1.377B 2026 notes, $2.75B 5.25% secured notes). The SpaceX leg ($8.5B cash + $8.5B/$11.1B equity) plus SpaceX-funded interim debt service (~$2B through Nov 2027, ~$3B through Nov 2028) is the bridge. Net read: a once-near-default credit now substantially de-risked on a forward basis (post-deal debt expected to fall to ~$13.4B with ~$10.7B net cash, and equity is a Fortune-500-leading SpaceX backdoor proxy via $11.1B of SpaceX stock), but the CREDIT remains fragile in the interim window — solvency depends on the bridge financing holding and the deals closing on schedule, and it is still managing coupons on 30-day grace mechanics as of the analysis date.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: moderate\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Q1 2026 10-Q filed substantial-doubt GOING-CONCERN language pending completion of AT&T/SpaceX deals; Deferred ~$183M cash interest on DISH DBS notes June 2, 2025 (5.25% '26, 5.75% '28, 5.125% '29); cured within 30-day grace period July 30, 2025; Deferred a further ~$183M interest payment in early June 2026 pending AT&T cash, again using grace-period mechanics; ~$24.1-24.6B principal debt vs ~$1.5B cash (Q1 2026); ~$6.2B maturing within 12 months; $16.48B nine-month 2025 impairments/other charges; Q3 2025 operating loss ~$16.6B (5G network + spectrum write-down); FCC inquiry/dispute over spectrum buildout obligations raised May 2025 (existential regulatory overhang, now substantially resolved via the asset sales); Repeated reliance on bridge financing and SpaceX-funded interim debt service to meet coupons; Total revenue declined ~5.8% YoY (Q2 2025); wireless OIBDA fell ~36.8% YoY; Pay-TV OIBDA -11.9% — secularly declining legacy cash flows\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? ((XBRL-negEBITDA), tier neg_ebitda_levered)\nTHESIS: The seed flag (neg EBITDA -$0.34B, $5.33B net debt) is a STALE, TRAILING-TROUGH snapshot that both understates the debt and mis-times the cycle. However, the fragility is MODERATING, not accelerating: Q1 2026 adjusted EBITDA turned positive (+$95M reported, ~$175M ex a one-time ~$80M energy spike), GAAP loss narrowed to -$229M from -$486M YoY, and management guides to positive free cash flow in Q2 2026 with sequential improvement. The company has actively de-risked — a ~$964M equity raise (Oct 2025) paid down the ABL, a ~$310M/yr cost-cut program with ~2,000 layoffs and plant closures (Steelton permanent-close early 2026), and an asset-sale process (JPMorgan-advised; idle mills marketed to data-center developers) targets billions in debt reduction. ### Krispy Kreme, Inc. ((XBRL-negEBITDA), tier neg_ebitda_levered)\nTHESIS: The seed flag's \"negative EBITDA, cash-burning AND levered\" is HALF an artifact and HALF real, and the mechanism is the opposite of the \"early-stage-with-cash-burn\" framing. ARTIFACT side: the XBRL \"$-0.33B EBITDA\" reads off the GAAP loss, which in FY2025 was dominated by ~$432M of NON-CASH goodwill/asset impairments (FY2025 net loss ~$516M). On an operating basis the company generates POSITIVE Adjusted EBITDA — $193.5M FY2024, $140.3M FY2025, ~$33M Q1 2026. So this is not a cash-burning startup; it is a mature, cash-generative-at-the-EBITDA-line franchise that took big impairment write-downs. REAL side: it is genuinely over-levered on a SHRINKING, low-margin business. Net debt ~$0.76-0.87B against falling EBITDA put management-defined net leverage at 6.7x (FY2025), with the dividend SUSPENDED in Q1 2025 explicitly citing 'negative free cash flow, high leverage, and negative interest coverage.' Two demand pillars collapsed in 2025: the McDonald's national rollout (2,400 locations) was terminated July 2025 over 'unsustainable operating costs,' and organic revenue keeps declining (-2.6% Q1 2026). Crucially, deleveraging is being funded by SELLING THE BUSINESS — Insomnia Cookies ($75M), Japan (~$70M), a Western-US JV (~$90M) — i.e. shrinking-to-deleverage, not earning-to-deleverage, which caps the upside and shrinks the asset base behind the debt. FRAGILITY IS REAL BUT NOT ACUTE/IMMINENT: the verdict is a levered, structurally-challenged turnaround, NOT a near-term default canary. Mitigants are strong: (1) NO near-term maturity wall — the 2023 facility (Term Loan A ~$732M + revolver) matures March 2028; near-term (<=2027) maturities ~$0, consistent with the scan; (2) the BANK-COVENANT 'Total Net Leverage Ratio' (a looser, add-back-heavy definition than the 6.7x management figure) was only 3.94x at Q1 2025 and is now <4x against a 5.00x covenant cap — ample covenant headroom, NOT a breach risk; (3) liquidity ~$303M and FCF turned modestly positive (+$11.4M) in Q1 2026; (4) leverage fell to 5.5x by Q1 2026. Net: moderate fragility driven by secular demand erosion + leverage, with a credible runway to 2028 and no imminent trigger. The 'going concern / cash-burning startup' read is REJECTED; the 'over-levered, shrinking, dividend-cut turnaround' read is CONFIRMED.\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: moderate\nDISTRESS INDICATORS CLAIMED: Dividend fully SUSPENDED Q1 2025, explicitly citing 'negative free cash flow, high leverage, and negative interest coverage'; FY2025 net loss ~$516M including ~$432M non-cash goodwill/asset impairments (FY2024 had only ~$4.5M impairment) — large write-down of carrying value; McDonald's USA partnership terminated July 2025 (~2,400 locations) over 'unsustainable operating costs'; Persistent quarterly GAAP net losses (Q1 2025 -$33.4M; Q1 2026 -$22.7M) and continuing organic revenue declines (-2.6% Q1 2026); Management net leverage spiked to 6.7x at FY2025 (Q4 2025); Deleveraging funded by asset disposals — Insomnia Cookies ($75M), Japan (~$70M), Western-US JV (~$90M) — shrinking-to-deleverage; Negative operating free cash flow of -$46.7M in Q1 2025; Capex cut ~66% YoY (to ~2.4% of revenue) — sign of cash conservation / underinvestment; Stock down sharply from IPO levels; turnaround plan announced Q2 2025\n\nAttack it: are the cash flows actually adequate? The flag triggered on FY2025 GAAP net loss of ~$546M (\"-$0.55B EBITDA\") and \"$0.90B gross debt / $0.84B net debt\" — both overstate distress. The \"$0.90B\" is the stale 2021 SoftBank face value; current convertible debt is ~$647M principal (2029: $200M @1.50%; 2030: $441M @1.375%), carrying ~$644M. With $276.0M cash+investments (Q1'26), net debt is ~$370M, NOT $840M. THAT SAID, genuine fragility is present: (1) total stockholders' equity has collapsed from $506.6M (YE2024) to $2.4M (Q1'26) — a near-zero-equity, deeply leveraged-on-converts capital structure; (2) the company has never been cash-flow positive; (3) Q1'26 operating cash USE was $44.7M (vs $111M for all of FY2025), implying burn is front-loaded / working-capital-heavy and runway on $276M cash is plausibly only ~6-10 quarters absent the improvement trajectory; (4) revenue is roughly flat ($160M FY25, guide $165-175M FY26) in a weak academic-spend environment, so the 'march to breakeven' depends on margin expansion and the new SPRQ-Nx/SparkNex launch executing. Mitigants are strong: low 1.375-1.5% coupons, NO maturities before Aug-2029 (near-term ≤2027 debt ≈ $0, confirming the scan), $48.1M short-read asset-sale cash inflow, narrowing losses (adj. EBITDA -$8.2M in Q1'26 vs much worse YoY), and no going-concern flag. Net: a cash-burning, near-zero-equity early-commercial company whose distress is genuine on the equity/burn axis but whose DEBT is well-laddered and cheap — not a near-term default canary. The retail casino base is solidly cash-generative: ~$1.9B segment adjusted EBITDAR in 2025 at ~32% margins. The bleeding digital wound is being cauterized: PENN and ESPN mutually terminated the OSB partnership effective Dec 1, 2025 (the deal cost ~$150M/yr; termination payment only ~$38–43M), and the rebranded theScore Bet / Hollywood iCasino realignment narrows the loss. ### LIVEPERSON INC ((XBRL-negEBITDA), tier neg_ebitda_levered)\nTHESIS: The seed flag (negative EBITDA + $0.41B net debt, cash-burning AND levered) is REAL on the operating side but STALE on the balance-sheet/solvency side, and the acute distress has been resolved by two events the scan missed. Operating reality: revenue is in deep secular decline (Q4-2025 $59.3M, -19% YoY; FY2025 guided ~$230-240M, -23/-26% YoY; FY2026 guided $195-207M, -15/-20% YoY) driven by customer cancellations/downsells; adjusted EBITDA is thin-to-negative (FY2026 guided -$4M to +$7M) and management explicitly states adjusted EBITDA-less-CapEx (a FCF proxy) will NOT be positive in 2026 — so the business is still a slow cash-burner. BUT the capital structure that generated the \"$0.51B gross / $0.41B net debt\" flag is the OLD 2020-vintage 0% Convertible Notes due 2026 ($517.5M originally). That stack has been almost entirely restructured: (a) June-2024 exchange of $146M of 2026 notes into $150M First-Lien 2029 Convertible Notes; (b) Sept-15-2025 deleveraging that exchanged $341.1M of 2026 notes for $45M cash + $115M of 10% Second-Lien Secured Notes due 2029 (PIK until Mar-2027) + ~39% equity dilution (Series B preferred + common), cutting net debt ~$226M and pushing maturities to 2029. Only a ~$20.1M 2026-note stub remains (due Dec-15-2026), easily covered by ~$95M YE-2025 cash. The seed's \"near-term (≤2027) ~$0.00B\" is therefore wrong (misses the $20M Dec-2026 stub) but directionally right that the wall is now small. Decisive overlay: on April-21-2026 SoundHound AI signed a DEFINITIVE all-stock agreement to acquire LivePerson (equity value ~$43M, ~22% premium; ~$250M EV; ~34.9M SOUN shares), under which first- and second-lien noteholders agreed to take SoundHound stock+cash \"in satisfaction of their claims,\" explicitly creating a DEBT-FREE combined group; expected close 2H-2026. Net: this is a declining, sub-scale, cash-burning software business — genuinely impaired as a standalone going concern — but the credit/solvency fragility that the tier ('neg_ebitda_levered') implies has been largely engineered away by restructuring and is being terminated by an acquisition that retires the debt.
